{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_eb86644f4e684131b2c9fdf2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Report usage across every tool round, not just the last\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#93\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:S, area:api\n5\t\n6\t## Why\n7\t\n8\t`afterModelResponse` is the hook a deployer reaches for when they need to record what a turn cost — an audit entry, a billing line, a usage budget. It is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n9\t\n10\tFor a turn that answered directly, that is the whole turn. For a turn that called tools, it is the last leg of several, and the earlier rounds — including the one carrying the customer's question and the full tool results — are not counted anywhere. A deployer billing or budgeting from this hook under-counts exactly the expensive turns, and nothing in the signature hints at it. The current behaviour is recorded in `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` so it is at least discoverable, but documenting a surprising number is weaker than not producing one.\n11\t\n12\tRaised by the reviewer on HALEngine#83, which recorded the behaviour but could not change it: that PR's ticket scoped itself to specifying the seam, not altering the orchestrator.\n13\t\n14\t## What to do\n15\t\n16\tDecide, and make the signature say the answer. Two shapes are worth weighing, and the issue should record why the other was rejected:\n17\t\n18\t- Accumulate across rounds and pass the sum, keeping one `usage` argument. Simple for the common case, and it silently changes what an existing consumer records — the number gets larger for tool-heavy turns, which is the point, but it is a behaviour change worth a MINOR bump and a changelog line that says so.\n19\t- Pass both, for example the summed total plus the per-round breakdown, so a consumer that wants provider-call granularity keeps it. More faithful, wider surface.\n20\t\n21\tWhichever is chosen, a turn that ran no tools must report exactly what it reports today, so the common path does not move.\n22\t\n23\t## Acceptance criteria\n24\t\n25\t- [ ] A turn driven through two tool rounds reports usage covering every provider call in that turn, asserted against a scripted provider whose rounds report known, different token counts\n26\t- [ ] A turn with no tool calls reports exactly what it reports today, pinned by the existing test remaining unedited\n27\t- [ ] A provider that reports no usage on some rounds does not make the total `undefined` or `NaN`: the rounds that reported are still counted, and a turn where nothing reported still reports nothing\n28\t- [ ] The type of what the hook receives makes the answer unambiguous, so a reader does not have to open the orchestrator to learn whether it is a sum\n29\t- [ ] `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` replaces its recorded under-counting line with the new behaviour, and the CHANGELOG entry states the change in what an existing consumer will record\n30\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`) and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the public API\n31\t\n32\t## Tech notes\n33\t\n34\tOut of scope: the audit entry or billing record itself, which is a consumer's concern; token accounting for anything other than the provider calls the orchestrator makes; and the `stop` chunk's own `usage` shape, which providers already populate and this issue only aggregates.\n35\t\n36\tKey files: `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` (the `lastUsage` local and the `afterModelResponse` call site), `src/types/ai.ts` (`UsageMetadata`), `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md`.\n37\t\n38\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6912dd03-3ad2-4cdd-8683-51058e28f456","uuid":"46cb9284-ea9a-4c4f-99f8-690c307370f5","timestamp":"2026-11-01T20:39:29.039Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/255b34f53d96/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Report usage across every tool round, not just the last\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#93\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:S, area:api\n\n## Why\n\n`afterModelResponse` is the hook a deployer reaches for when they need to record what a turn cost — an audit entry, a billing line, a usage budget. It is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n\nFor a turn that answered directly, that is the whole turn. For a turn that called tools, it is the last leg of several, and the earlier rounds — including the one carrying the customer's question and the full tool results — are not counted anywhere. A deployer billing or budgeting from this hook under-counts exactly the expensive turns, and nothing in the signature hints at it. The current behaviour is recorded in `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` so it is at least discoverable, but documenting a surprising number is weaker than not producing one.\n\nRaised by the reviewer on HALEngine#83, which recorded the behaviour but could not change it: that PR's ticket scoped itself to specifying the seam, not altering the orchestrator.\n\n## What to do\n\nDecide, and make the signature say the answer. Two shapes are worth weighing, and the issue should record why the other was rejected:\n\n- Accumulate across rounds and pass the sum, keeping one `usage` argument. Simple for the common case, and it silently changes what an existing consumer records — the number gets larger for tool-heavy turns, which is the point, but it is a behaviour change worth a MINOR bump and a changelog line that says so.\n- Pass both, for example the summed total plus the per-round breakdown, so a consumer that wants provider-call granularity keeps it. More faithful, wider surface.\n\nWhichever is chosen, a turn that ran no tools must report exactly what it reports today, so the common path does not move.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A turn driven through two tool rounds reports usage covering every provider call in that turn, asserted against a scripted provider whose rounds report known, different token counts\n- [ ] A turn with no tool calls reports exactly what it reports today, pinned by the existing test remaining unedited\n- [ ] A provider that reports no usage on some rounds does not make the total `undefined` or `NaN`: the rounds that reported are still counted, and a turn where nothing reported still reports nothing\n- [ ] The type of what the hook receives makes the answer unambiguous, so a reader does not have to open the orchestrator to learn whether it is a sum\n- [ ] `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` replaces its recorded under-counting line with the new behaviour, and the CHANGELOG entry states the change in what an existing consumer will record\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`) and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the public API\n\n## Tech notes\n\nOut of scope: the audit entry or billing record itself, which is a consumer's concern; token accounting for anything other than the provider calls the orchestrator makes; and the `stop` chunk's own `usage` shape, which providers already populate and this issue only aggregates.\n\nKey files: `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` (the `lastUsage` local and the `afterModelResponse` call site), `src/types/ai.ts` (`UsageMetadata`), `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md`.\n\n","numLines":38,"startLine":1,"totalLines":38}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_eb86644f4e684131b2c9fdf2","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_a5c7438f9a2d47a98f97b319","type":"tool_result","content":"commit 4ab8e096a84fe592fa2054a4b9bf3f3e70f5fce6\nAuthor: loop-bench <loop-bench@localhost>\nDate:   Sun Nov 1 21:34:26 2026 +0100\n\n    test(orchestrator): pin turn usage across tool rounds\n    \n    Acceptance tests for re-cinq/HALEngine#93: the usage handed to\n    afterModelResponse must sum every provider call of the turn, not the\n    last round alone. The spec's recorded under-counting line is replaced by the\n    specified behaviour, cited to the new tests, and .lore/dod.md records\n    the signature decision.\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Code <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\n .lore/dod.md                                       |  34 +++++++\n specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md              |  12 ++-\n .../chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts      | 110 +++++++++++++++++++++\n 3 files changed, 154 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)\n----\n867ad52 Merge pull request #88 from re-cinq/dependabot/npm_and_yarn/types/cookie-1.0.0\nbc26def Merge pull request #98 from re-cinq/lore/implementation-loop/issue-94\n3a04319 fix(orchestrator): re-anchor spec citations after merging main","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_a5c7438f9a2d47a98f97b319","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_32c8cd7509a242ddad2cb623","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n2\t\n3\t| Field  | Value                |\n4\t| ------ | -------------------- |\n5\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n6\t| Status | Shipped              |\n7\t\n8\t`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n9\t\n10\t## Installing hooks through config\n11\t\n12\t- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n13\t- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n14\t- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n15\t- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n16\t- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n17\t- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n18\t\n19\t## Usage reported to afterModelResponse\n20\t\n21\t`afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L148](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L148)), and what it receives as `usage` is the turn's total, specified below rather than recorded.\n22\t\n23\t- The `usage` it receives sums every provider call of the turn, so a turn that ran tools is counted whole and a deployer billing or budgeting from the hook is not handed only the last leg ([validated by: reports the sum of every round that reported, not the last round alone](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts#L77)).\n24\t- A round whose provider reported no usage contributes nothing to that total rather than ending it: the rounds that did report are still summed ([validated by: keeps counting the rounds that reported when a round in between reports none](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts#L89)).\n25\t- A turn whose rounds report no usage at all still reports no usage ([validated by: still reports nothing when no round in the turn reported usage](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts#L102)).\n26\t- A turn that ran no tools reports exactly what it has always reported — the one provider call's usage — so the common path does not move ([validated by: receives collected response text and usage](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L236)).\n27\t\n28\t## Engine startup\n29\t\n30\t- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n31\t\n32\t## Measured semantics — out of scope\n33\t\n34\tThis property of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` is recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. No issue in this epic owns changing it.\n35\t\n36\t- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n37\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6912dd03-3ad2-4cdd-8683-51058e28f456","uuid":"2a8ff952-c665-4fb4-9b17-3ad57f5f8254","timestamp":"2026-11-01T20:39:35.774Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/255b34f53d96/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md","content":"# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n| Status | Shipped              |\n\n`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n\n## Installing hooks through config\n\n- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n\n## Usage reported to afterModelResponse\n\n`afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L148](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L148)), and what it receives as `usage` is the turn's total, specified below rather than recorded.\n\n- The `usage` it receives sums every provider call of the turn, so a turn that ran tools is counted whole and a deployer billing or budgeting from the hook is not handed only the last leg ([validated by: reports the sum of every round that reported, not the last round alone](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts#L77)).\n- A round whose provider reported no usage contributes nothing to that total rather than ending it: the rounds that did report are still summed ([validated by: keeps counting the rounds that reported when a round in between reports none](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts#L89)).\n- A turn whose rounds report no usage at all still reports no usage ([validated by: still reports nothing when no round in the turn reported usage](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts#L102)).\n- A turn that ran no tools reports exactly what it has always reported — the one provider call's usage — so the common path does not move ([validated by: receives collected response text and usage](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L236)).\n\n## Engine startup\n\n- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n\n## Measured semantics — out of scope\n\nThis property of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` is recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. No issue in this epic owns changing it.\n\n- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n","numLines":37,"startLine":1,"totalLines":37}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_32c8cd7509a242ddad2cb623","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_e14f3330cdfb4f0d8fc66f49","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Definition of Done\n2\t\n3\t> `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n4\t\n5\t**Strategy: `direct`** — the seam already exists: `createChatOrchestrator` takes any `AIProvider`, so a scripted provider drives real tool rounds through the real round loop and the hook's third argument is observable. The acceptance tests call `processMessage` and fail on the usage the hook received, not on a missing seam.\n6\t\n7\t**Signature decision** (the issue asks that it be recorded): accumulate across rounds and keep ONE `usage` argument — the sum over the rounds that reported — typed by a new dedicated exported type (name it `TurnUsage`; three `UsageMetadata`-shaped number fields) whose declaration states it is the turn's total, declared in `src/types/ai.ts` and re-exported through `src/types/index.ts` and `src/index.ts`. The per-round-breakdown shape is rejected: criterion 2 pins the existing no-tool test unedited, and any wrapped object (sum + `rounds` fields) breaks its `toEqual`; no consumer of a breakdown is named in the issue; per-call granularity stays available on each round's `stop` chunk, which the orchestrator already streams through. A fourth positional parameter is rejected with it: wider surface, no stated need. The MINOR bump and changelog line are still owed, because the number an existing consumer records grows on exactly the tool-heavy turns.\n8\t\n9\t## Done when these pass\n10\t\n11\t- [ ] **reports the sum of every round that reported, not the last round alone** — a turn driven through two tool rounds reports usage covering every provider call: 120/35/155 plus 40/18/58 arrives as 160/53/213, not the last round's 40/18/58 (red today)\n12\t  `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts`\n13\t- [ ] **keeps counting the rounds that reported when a round in between reports none** — a silent round neither zeroes, NaNs nor ends the total; the reporting rounds around it are still summed (red today)\n14\t  `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts`\n15\t- [ ] **still reports nothing when no round in the turn reported usage** — a guard, green today and it must stay so: the sum must not surface as `{0,0,0}` when no round reported\n16\t  `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts`\n17\t\n18\t## Facets\n19\t\n20\t- [ ] `chatOrchestrator.ts`: replace the `lastUsage` local with a turn total accumulated across rounds — each field summed over the rounds that reported `usage`, a round that reported none adding nothing, and a turn where nothing reported still passing `undefined` — and pass that to `afterModelResponse`\n21\t- [ ] Type the third argument with the new dedicated type and give its declaration a one-line JSDoc saying it is the sum across the turn's provider calls. This is acceptance criterion 4 and it has no red test on purpose: TS is structural, so a renamed type identical to `UsageMetadata` cannot be distinguished at runtime; review and `npm run typecheck` hold it\n22\t- [ ] Run `node scripts/check-doc-blocks.mjs --fix` after touching the declaration: the `OrchestratorHooks` block is generated into `CLAUDE.md` and `specs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md`, and CI's `npm run docs:check` compares the blocks to the source byte-for-byte\n23\t- [ ] The spec's new section `Usage reported to afterModelResponse` (committed with these tests) becomes true; its citations already point at the acceptance tests, and if the orchestrator edit shifts test lines, repoint with `npm run spec:names:fix`; `npm run check:spec-links` and `npm run check:spec-status` are the gates\n24\t- [ ] CHANGELOG: under `## [Unreleased]` → `### Changed`, one entry stating the change in what an existing consumer records (the `usage` argument is now the turn's summed total, larger on tool-heavy turns) with a migration note; MINOR bump per the repo's behaviour-change rules\n25\t- [ ] `afterModelResponse > receives collected response text and usage` in `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts` — acceptance criterion 2 — stays UNEDITED and green. No existing test anywhere needs editing: none drives two reporting rounds through the hook\n26\t- [ ] Then CI's checklist: `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`), `npm run prettier:check`, no `any` in the public API\n27\t\n28\t## Out of scope\n29\t\n30\t- The audit entry or billing record itself — a consumer's concern, per the issue\n31\t- Token accounting for anything other than the provider calls the orchestrator makes\n32\t- The `stop` chunk's own `usage` shape — providers already populate it; this issue only aggregates\n33\t- A per-round breakdown in the hook signature — rejected, see the signature decision above\n34\t- README, `docs/getting-started.md` and `example/` hook snippets — they log whatever arrives and stay truthful under the sum\n35\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6912dd03-3ad2-4cdd-8683-51058e28f456","uuid":"1bc2dfc4-1f29-49a7-8629-0e47cb498640","timestamp":"2026-11-01T20:39:42.573Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/255b34f53d96/workspace/target/.lore/dod.md","content":"# Definition of Done\n\n> `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — the seam already exists: `createChatOrchestrator` takes any `AIProvider`, so a scripted provider drives real tool rounds through the real round loop and the hook's third argument is observable. The MINOR bump and changelog line are still owed, because the number an existing consumer records grows on exactly the tool-heavy turns.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **reports the sum of every round that reported, not the last round alone** — a turn driven through two tool rounds reports usage covering every provider call: 120/35/155 plus 40/18/58 arrives as 160/53/213, not the last round's 40/18/58 (red today)\n  `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **keeps counting the rounds that reported when a round in between reports none** — a silent round neither zeroes, NaNs nor ends the total; the reporting rounds around it are still summed (red today)\n  `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **still reports nothing when no round in the turn reported usage** — a guard, green today and it must stay so: the sum must not surface as `{0,0,0}` when no round reported\n  `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.usage.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] `chatOrchestrator.ts`: replace the `lastUsage` local with a turn total accumulated across rounds — each field summed over the rounds that reported `usage`, a round that reported none adding nothing, and a turn where nothing reported still passing `undefined` — and pass that to `afterModelResponse`\n- [ ] Type the third argument with the new dedicated type and give its declaration a one-line JSDoc saying it is the sum across the turn's provider calls. This is acceptance criterion 4 and it has no red test on purpose: TS is structural, so a renamed type identical to `UsageMetadata` cannot be distinguished at runtime; review and `npm run typecheck` hold it\n- [ ] Run `node scripts/check-doc-blocks.mjs --fix` after touching the declaration: the `OrchestratorHooks` block is generated into `CLAUDE.md` and `specs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md`, and CI's `npm run docs:check` compares the blocks to the source byte-for-byte\n- [ ] The spec's new section `Usage reported to afterModelResponse` (committed with these tests) becomes true; its citations already point at the acceptance tests, and if the orchestrator edit shifts test lines, repoint with `npm run spec:names:fix`; `npm run check:spec-links` and `npm run check:spec-status` are the gates\n- [ ] CHANGELOG: under `## [Unreleased]` → `### Changed`, one entry stating the change in what an existing consumer records (the `usage` argument is now the turn's summed total, larger on tool-heavy turns) with a migration note; MINOR bump per the repo's behaviour-change rules\n- [ ] `afterModelResponse > receives collected response text and usage` in `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts` — acceptance criterion 2 — stays UNEDITED and green. No existing test anywhere needs editing: none drives two reporting rounds through the hook\n- [ ] Then CI's checklist: `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`), `npm run prettier:check`, no `any` in the public API\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- The audit entry or billing record itself — a consumer's concern, per the issue\n- Token accounting for anything other than the provider calls the orchestrator makes\n- The `stop` chunk's own `usage` shape — providers already populate it; this issue only aggregates\n- A per-round breakdown in the hook signature — rejected, see the signature decision above\n- README, `docs/getting-started.md` and `example/` hook snippets — they log whatever arrives and stay truthful under the sum\n","numLines":35,"startLine":1,"totalLines":35}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_e14f3330cdfb4f0d8fc66f49","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_21ae467d59bc494e8a265b6a","type":"tool_result","content":"#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Holds every `([validated by: <test name>](path#Lnn))` citation to the test it names.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs           # report, exit 1 on any finding\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs --fix     # repoint each line from the name it carries\n//\n// A line number is the wrong thing to cite: inserting an import above a suite moves every test below\n// it, and the citation silently lands on a different, still-valid declaration. The name is what the\n// author meant, so the name is what the citation records and the line becomes a derived pointer this\n// script repairs. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job both index by it, and a\n// citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than one test.\nimport {readFileSync, writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport process from 'node:process';\nimport {dirname, join, normalize} from 'node:path';\nimport {root} from './lib/repo-root.mjs';\n\nconst CITATION = /\\[validated by(?<label>: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>(?:\\.\\.\\/)+[\\w./-]+?)#L(?<line>\\d+)\\)/g;\n// Any other href shape is refused rather than ignored: the coverage job resolves `./../x` and `/x`\n// to the same file and counts them, while this gate and the drift check would never have read them.\nconst ANY_HREF = /\\[validated by(?:: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>[^)#\\s]+)#L\\d+\\)/g;\n// `xit`, `xdescribe` and `.skip` declare a test that never runs, which validates nothing.\nconst DECLARATION = /^\\s*(?:f?it|f?describe|test)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst SKIPPED = /^\\s*(?:x(?:it|describe|test)|(?:it|describe|test)\\.skip)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst TEST_FILE = /\\.(?:test|spec)\\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/;\n\nconst cache = new Map();\nconst args = process.argv.slice(2);\nconst fix = args.includes('--fix');\nconst named = args.filter(arg => !arg.startsWith('--'));\nconst findings = [];\nlet checked = 0;\nlet repaired = 0;\n\nfor (const spec of named.length > 0 ? named : specs()) {\n  const source = readFileSync(join(root, spec), 'utf8');\n  const specDir = dirname(spec);\n  let changed = false;","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6912dd03-3ad2-4cdd-8683-51058e28f456","uuid":"3d01ce76-7828-482b-bb94-03124837366f","timestamp":"2026-11-01T20:40:35.697Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Holds every `([validated by: <test name>](path#Lnn))` citation to the test it names.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs           # report, exit 1 on any finding\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs --fix     # repoint each line from the name it carries\n//\n// A line number is the wrong thing to cite: inserting an import above a suite moves every test below\n// it, and the citation silently lands on a different, still-valid declaration. The name is what the\n// author meant, so the name is what the citation records and the line becomes a derived pointer this\n// script repairs. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job both index by it, and a\n// citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than one test.\nimport {readFileSync, writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport process from 'node:process';\nimport {dirname, join, normalize} from 'node:path';\nimport {root} from './lib/repo-root.mjs';\n\nconst CITATION = /\\[validated by(?<label>: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>(?:\\.\\.\\/)+[\\w./-]+?)#L(?<line>\\d+)\\)/g;\n// Any other href shape is refused rather than ignored: the coverage job resolves `./../x` and `/x`\n// to the same file and counts them, while this gate and the drift check would never have read them.\nconst ANY_HREF = /\\[validated by(?:: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>[^)#\\s]+)#L\\d+\\)/g;\n// `xit`, `xdescribe` and `.skip` declare a test that never runs, which validates nothing.\nconst DECLARATION = /^\\s*(?:f?it|f?describe|test)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst SKIPPED = /^\\s*(?:x(?:it|describe|test)|(?:it|describe|test)\\.skip)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst TEST_FILE = /\\.(?:test|spec)\\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/;\n\nconst cache = new Map();\nconst args = process.argv.slice(2);\nconst fix = args.includes('--fix');\nconst named = args.filter(arg => !arg.startsWith('--'));\nconst findings = [];\nlet checked = 0;\nlet repaired = 0;\n\nfor (const spec of named.length > 0 ? named : specs()) {\n  const source = readFileSync(join(root, spec), 'utf8');\n  const specDir = dirname(spec);\n  let changed = false;","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_21ae467d59bc494e8a265b6a","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
